Hi, Doctor. Thank you for fitting me in. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time. I'm sure you deal with much more serious things, but my wife thought I should come talk to someone. 

I guess the main thing is that I've just... stopped caring about everything I used to look forward to. Which sounds terrible, I know, I shouldn't be complaining. But, like, my fishing gear is just sitting untouched out in the garage. And my woodshop—I usually love working out there, but lately it just feels like a massive chore. 

And, um... I really hate to even admit this, because I know I should be doing better for them, but even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I'm really just going through the motions. I try to put on a good face so they don't worry, of course. Um, I hope I'm explaining this right... is that the kind of information you're looking for?

Well, um... I suppose it's been a while. I really hate to admit it's been this long, because a guy my age should have snapped out of it by now, you know? But I'd say it's been going on for about a year and seven months.

Well, um... maybe a little bit? I mean, I know I'm forty-five now, so I'm sure some of it is just normal wear and tear on the brain, you know? But... well, I really hate to admit this, because I've always prided myself on being really sharp at work. I'm a construction manager, and I used to be able to keep all the project schedules and subcontractors straight right in my head. No problem at all. 

But lately... I don't want to sound incompetent, but I'll find myself reading the exact same email three times before the words actually register. And, um, in meetings, I'll sometimes just completely forget what I was about to say right in the middle of a sentence. The hardest part, though, is the choices. Things I used to make a call on in five minutes... I just sort of freeze up over them now. I try really hard to cover it up so the crew and the clients don't lose confidence in me, but it's taking a lot of energy. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible at my job. I really am trying my absolute best to stay on top of things for everyone. Is that... does that answer what you were asking about?

At home I’m present physically, but my wife and kids are having to work around me instead of being able to count on me the way they used to.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and seven months, since my wife’s health problems changed everything at home.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 45 / construction_manager / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S025\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what’s been bothering you or what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you for fitting me in. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time. I'm sure you deal with much more serious things, but my wife thought I should come talk to someone. \n\nI guess the main thing is that I've just... stopped caring about everything I used to look forward to. Which sounds terrible, I know, I shouldn't be complaining. But, like, my fishing gear is just sitting untouched out in the garage. I try to put on a good face so they don't worry, of course. I suppose it's been a while. I really hate to admit it's been this long, because a guy my age should have snapped out of it by now, you know? I really should be stronger for her. Um, does that answer your question? I mean, I know I'm forty-five now, so I'm sure some of it is just normal wear and tear on the brain, you know? But... well, I really hate to admit this, because I've always prided myself on being really sharp at work. I'm a construction manager, and I used to be able to keep all the project schedules and subcontractors straight right in my head. No problem at all. \n\nBut lately... I don't want to sound incompetent, but I'll find myself reading the exact same email three times before the words actually register. And, um, in meetings, I'll sometimes just completely forget what I was about to say right in the middle of a sentence. The hardest part, though, is the choices. Things I used to make a call on in five minutes... I just sort of freeze up over them now. I try really hard to cover it up so the crew and the clients don't lose confidence in me, but it's taking a lot of energy. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terrible at my job. I really am trying my absolute best to stay on top of things for everyone. Well, um... I guess sleep has been a little tricky lately. I mean, it's probably just stress from the job, and I'm sure plenty of guys my age don't sleep like they used to. I really don't want to make a big deal out of it. 

But, um... yeah, I can get into bed just absolutely exhausted, you know? But then I either just lie there running through bills and work schedules in my head, or... well, or I'll wake up around three or four in the morning. I try to stay quiet so I don't wake my wife, because she really needs her rest. I know I should just be able to turn my brain off and sleep like a normal person. I hope I'm not complaining too much about something so basic... is that the kind of detail you needed?

Well, um... I guess my appetite hasn't been the greatest lately. I mean, I'm sure a lot of guys my age just naturally lose that big appetite they had in their twenties, so I really don't want to make it sound like a bigger deal than it is. 

But... yeah, to be honest, most days food just kind of tastes like cardboard to me now. So I'll usually just skip breakfast, and then... well, even if I've been running around a job site all day and should be starving, I just sort of pick at my dinner. I really should be eating properly to keep my strength up for my family and the crew.

Well, um... no, I don't think I have. I mean, I've always been a pretty steady guy, you know? Sure, everyone gets stressed out when a project is running behind or budgets get tight, but I've always just been able to shake it off and get the job done.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself, I promise. I know how selfish that would be, especially with my wife needing me and the guys counting on me at work. I know I have responsibilities, and I take them very seriously. I'd never want you to think I'm the kind of guy who just gives up.

But, um... I guess... well, sometimes when I'm driving to the site... you know, those really early mornings before the sun is up and no one else is on the road... I just get these thoughts. I catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. 

And, um, sometimes... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road kind of pops into my head. It actually scares me a little bit, to be honest, because it feels a little too close, if that makes sense? But I always grip the wheel tighter and keep driving. I would never actually do it. I just... I feel terrible even admitting that I think about it. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that. Does... does that make sense?

So I'll usually just skip breakfast, and then... well, even if I've been running around a job site all day and should be starving, I just sort of pick at my dinner. Does... does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or have others commented that you seem slowed down, or alternatively, have you felt restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Doc, I... I feel terrible, I know we just went over this and you're asking again, so I must be giving you the wrong answer. I'm really, really sorry if I'm frustrating you or making your job difficult. I promise I'm not trying to be stubborn or hide anything from you.

I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of details.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... maybe a little bit? I mean, I know I'm forty-five now, so I'm sure some of it is just normal wear and tear on the brain, you know? But... well, I really hate to admit this, because I've always prided myself on being really sharp at work. I'm a construction manager, and I used to be able to keep all the project schedules and subcontractors straight right in my head. No problem at all. \n\nBut lately... I don't want to sound incompetent, but I'll find myself reading the exact same email three times before the words actually register. I try really hard to cover it up so the crew and the clients don't lose confidence in me, but it's taking a lot of energy. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terrible at my job. I really am trying my absolute best to stay on top of things for everyone. I know I should just be able to turn my brain off and sleep like a normal person. But then I either just lie there running through bills and work schedules in my head, or... well, or I'll wake up around three or four in the morning. And then I just sort of stare at the ceiling until it's time to drive to the site. \n\nI try to stay quiet so I don't wake my wife, because she really needs her rest. I know I should just be able to turn my brain off and sleep like a normal person. I hope I'm not complaining too much about something so basic... is that the kind of detail you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, such as a significant loss of appetite or unintentional weight loss or gain?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my appetite hasn't been the greatest lately. I mean, I'm sure a lot of guys my age just naturally lose that big appetite they had in their twenties, so I really don't want to make it sound like a bigger deal than it is. \n\nBut... yeah, to be honest, most days food just kind of tastes like cardboard to me now. So I'll usually just skip breakfast, and then... well, even if I've been running around a job site all day and should be starving, I just sort of pick at my dinner. I feel awful about it because I don't want my wife to think I don't appreciate her cooking, especially when she's the one who's been dealing with health stuff. I really should be eating properly to keep my strength up for my family and the crew. \n\nAnd, um, I guess I have lost some weight because of it. I've had to bring my work belt in a couple of notches recently without really trying to. I hope that doesn't mean I'm neglecting my health too badly. I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. Does... does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or have others commented that you seem slowed down, or alternatively, have you felt restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm definitely a little tired, like I mentioned with the sleep stuff, but I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. And I haven't been pacing the floors or feeling all wound up, either. \n\nI try to keep a pretty steady pace at work so the guys don't think I'm slacking off. I mean, you can't really afford to drag your feet on a construction site, you know? You have to set the tone for everyone else. So I just push through and keep moving like I always do. \n\nNo one has said anything to me about it, anyway. I hope I'm not missing something obvious that I should be noticing. But as far as I can tell, physically, I'm getting around just fine. Is that... is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods like this before, where you felt down or lost interest in things, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I have. I mean, I've always been a pretty steady guy, you know? Sure, everyone gets stressed out when a project is running behind or budgets get tight, but I've always just been able to shake it off and get the job done. \n\nSo... yeah, this is really the first time I've felt quite like this. Just feeling so... I don't know, flat, I guess? And not really caring about the stuff I normally enjoy. I've always prided myself on being the reliable one, the guy my wife and the crew can lean on, so it feels a little embarrassing to even admit that I'm struggling with it now. I really feel like I should just be able to snap out of it and get back to normal. \n\nI've never been stuck in a rut like this before. I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here by complaining about it... is that the kind of background you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or ending your life, or have you been preoccupied with thoughts about death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself, I promise. I know how selfish that would be, especially with my wife needing me and the guys counting on me at work. I know I have responsibilities, and I take them very seriously. I'd never want you to think I'm the kind of guy who just gives up.\n\nBut, um... I guess... well, sometimes when I'm driving to the site... you know, those really early mornings before the sun is up and no one else is on the road... I just get these thoughts. I catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road kind of pops into my head. It actually scares me a little bit, to be honest, because it feels a little too close, if that makes sense? But I always grip the wheel tighter and keep driving. I would never actually do it. I just... I feel terrible even admitting that I think about it. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that. Does... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for a period of time, even if you didn’t talk to anyone about it at the time?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I know we kind of just touched on that a minute ago, and I really want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... but, no, I honestly don't think so. \n\nEven looking back at times when maybe I kept things to myself... I mean, sure, there were tough weeks on the job where I felt stressed, but I never felt hopeless or stopped caring about my life. I always just put my head down, focused on providing for my wife, and pushed through it. \n\nI hope I'm not being unhelpful or missing something from my past that I'm supposed to be telling you. I'm trying really hard to think if there was ever a time, but honestly, this whole experience is completely new territory for me. I'm sorry if I'm just repeating myself... I really hope that's an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Looking back even further, have you ever had any periods in your life where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks at a time, even if you didn’t talk to anyone about it at the time?\n\nPatient: Gosh, um... I always just dusted myself off and got back to work. I never lost interest in my life or felt hopeless like I do right now. \n\nI'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the history you're looking for, or if it makes your job harder to figure out what's wrong with me. I'm just trying to be completely straight with you, and I promise I'm not trying to hide anything. I feel like I'm failing a test or something by not giving you the answer you're looking for. Am I... am I doing this okay?
